**no** whisker motion energy at all | 14 |\n   156→| Sessions with wheel data | 459 |\n   157→\n   158→---\n   159→\n   160→## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n   161→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   162→\n   163→### Expected Statistics (from papers/methods)\n   164→| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n   165→|-----------|-------|--------------|\n   166→| Units (total) | 621,733 | \"This process produced 621,733 units (including multineuron activity), averaging 889 per probe.\" (data paper) |\n   167→| Well-isolated neurons | 75,708 (108/probe) | \"…identified 75,708 well-isolated neurons, averaging 108 per probe.\" |\n   168→| Probes / sessions | 699 insertions, 459 sessions | \"a total of 459 sessions, 699 insertions and 621,733 neurons remained, constituting the publicly released dataset\" |\n   169→| Subjects | 139 mice (94 M, 45 F) | \"We trained 139 mice … on the IBL decision-making task\" |\n   170→| Brain areas | 279 (Beryl) | \"The probes covered 279 brain areas\" |\n   171→| Trials / session | mean 645, median 602, range 401–1,525 | \"Recorded sessions lasted on average 645 trials (median of 602, range of 401–1,525)\" |\n   172→| Proportion correct | 81.4 ± 0.4 % | \"they made correct choices on 81.4 ± 0.4% … of the trials\" |\n   173→| Reward on 0 %-contrast trials | 58.7 ± 0.4 % | \"On 0% contrast trials … mice gained rewards on 58.7 ± 0.4%\" |\n   174→| Reaction times < 80 ms | 22.8 % of trials | \"A total of 22.8% first wheel-movement times occurred under 80 ms\" |\n   175→| Block structure | first 90 trials p(left)=0.5, then 0.2/0.8 blocks of 20–100 trials (mean 51) | data paper, \"Methods\" |\n   176→| Sessions used by method paper | 433 (of 459) | \"On 433 sessions spanning 270 brain regions…\" |\n   177→| Neurons / session (method paper) | \"average of 676 … ranging from approximately 300 to over 2000\" | method paper, STAR Methods |\n   178→| Trial length / binning | 2 s trials, 20 ms bins, T = 100 | \"Recordings are split into 2-s trials, each divided into 20-ms bins, producing T = 100 time steps.\" |\n   179→| Alignment for choice/prior | stimulus onset, −0.5 s → +1.5 s | \"For choice, we align trials to the stimulus onset, considering neural activity from 0.5 s before to 1.5 s post-onset.\" |\n   180→| Behaviour sampling | wheel/whisker sampled at 60 Hz (video), wheel interpolated to 1 kHz | \"wheel speed and whisker motion energy are time-varying signals sampled at 60 Hz\" |\n   181→\n   182→### Processing Details\n   183→- **Temporal alignment**: `stimOn_times`; window (−0.5, +1.5) s; 20 ms non-overlapping bins;\n   184→  T = 100 (reference `params` in `0_data_caching.py`; matches the decoder task's request to\n   185→  \"temporally align based on stimulus onset\").\n   186→- `methods.txt` also contains the sentence \"Within each trial, we segment neural activity into\n   187→  50-ms non-overlapping time bins\" for choice/prior. This contradicts both the released code\n   188→  (`binsize: 0.02`) and the paper's own \"2-s trials … 20-ms bins … T = 100\". **20 ms is used**,\n   189→  which is also required here because two of the four decoder outputs are time-varying\n   190→  behaviours that must be resolved within the trial.\n   191→- **Whisker motion energy** = mean absolute difference between adjacent video frames in a\n   192→  bounding box anchored between nose tip and eye (left camera preferred, 60 Hz).\n   193→- **Wheel speed** = |wheel velocity|, from `SessionLoader.load_wheel()` (Gaussian-smoothed\n   194→  velocity on a 1 kHz uniform grid).\n   195→\n   196→### Curation Steps\n   197→\n   198→**Session curation (already applied by the release)**: ≥ 250 trials performed, ≥ 90 % correct on\n   199→100 %-contrast trials in both block types, ≥ 3 incorrect trials, hardware QC passed, probe\n   200→alignment resolved, no major artefacts. The 459-session / 699-pid freeze in `bwm_release.csv`\n   201→*is* the result of these criteria, so no further session-level QC is re-derived here.\n   202→\n   203→**Neuron curation rules** (data paper, \"Neurons and brain regions\"):\n   204→- exclude units failing any of the three RIGOR single-unit metrics (amplitude > 50 µV,\n   205→  noise cut-off < 20 µV, refractory-period violation) ⇒ keep `clusters.label >= 1`\n   206→  (\"well-isolated neurons\", 75,708 of 621,733);\n   207→- restrict to regions designated **grey matter** in the Allen CCF ⇒ drop units whose Beryl\n   208→  acronym is `root` (in brain, unassigned) or `void` (outside brain).\n   209→\n   210→**Trial curation rules** (data paper \"Trials\" = `load_trials_and_mask` defaults):\n   211→- drop trials with an undetected `choice`, `probabilityLeft`, `feedbackType`, `feedback_times`,\n   212→  `stimOn_times` or `firstMovement_times`;\n   213→- drop trials whose reaction time `firstMovement_times − stimOn_times` is outside 0.08–2.00 s;\n   214→- drop no-response trials (`choice == 0`);\n   215→- (reference code, `prepare_data`) drop trials with `feedback_times − goCue_times > 10 s`;\n   216→- (reference code, `align_spike_behavior`) drop trials for which the wheel or whisker trace does\n   217→  not cover the full −0.5…+1.5 s window (e.g. the first trials of a session, before the video\n   218→  starts).\n   219→\n   220→Measured on 15 sessions: 27.1 % of trials have RT < 80 ms, 7.8 % RT > 2 s, 1.6 % have a NaN\n   221→event, 0.5 % are no-go, 4.0 % have trial length > 10 s ⇒ ≈ 63 % of trials survive the trial-event\n   222→criteria (before the behaviour-coverage criterion).\n   223→\n   224→### Decoders Trained (reference numbers; different decoders/metrics than the one used here)\n   225→| Decoded variable | Reported performance |\n   226→|---|---|\n   227→| Choice (binary, single-session linear baseline / RRR, 10 sessions, 5 regions) | ≈ 0.6–0.8 accuracy / AUC (Figure 3A, bar plots; RRR ≳ linear) |\n   228→| Prior / block (BMM-HMM vs baseline) | AUC 0.72 (single-session) vs 0.79 (multi-session), baseline 0.66 |\n   229→| Wheel speed (R², aligned to first movement) | 0.24 → 0.55 (single-trial R², linear → RRR (M)) |\n   230→| Whisker motion energy (R²) | 0.52 → 0.80 |\n   231→| BWM paper, per-region decoding | null-corrected **median balanced accuracy**, small effect sizes (≈ 0.5–0.6 raw) for single regions |\n   232→\n   233→Note the reference decoders are region-specific/session-specific and use different metrics\n   234→(R²/AUC), so they are not directly comparable to the balanced accuracy produced by\n   235→`train_decoder.py`, which decodes from *all* neurons of a session at every time bin. They do\n   236→establish that choice/prior/wheel/whisker are all decodable well above chance from IBL\n   237→population activity.\n   238→\n   239→---\n   240→\n   241→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   242→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   243→\n   244→### Discrepancies Found\n   245→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n   246→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   247→| Time bin size | `binsize = 0.02` | n/a | method paper body: \"20-ms bins, T = 100\"; methods.txt STAR section: \"50-ms bins\" for choice/prior | Use **20 ms** (code + paper body); needed for time-varying outputs. Documented. |\n   248→| Alignment window | `time_window = (-0.5, 1.5)`, `align_time='stimOn_times'` | n/a | \"0.5 s before to 1.5 s post-onset\" for choice | Consistent → (−0.5, +1.5) s. |\n   249→| Neuron QC | `load_spiking_data(qc=None)` used in `prepare_data` (all units); `qc=1` supported | 621,733 units, 75,708 with `label>=1` | data paper: exclude units failing RIGOR ⇒ 75,708 well-isolated | Use **`label >= 1`** (see Step 5 rationale). Documented as a deliberate deviation from the reference caching script. |\n   250→| Neurons/session | all units ⇒ 1,354/session | 1,354 (all) / 165 (good) per session | method paper: \"average of 676 … 300 to over 2000\" | The method paper's 676 matches neither all-units (1,354) nor good-units (165) for the full 459-session freeze; its stated *range* (300–2,000) matches all-units. Treated as an unreconcilable paper statistic; not used as a target. |\n   251→| Sessions | `bwm_release.csv` → 459 | 459 staged | data paper 459; method paper analysed 433 | 459 is the release; sessions are dropped only when a required signal is missing (see Step 5). |\n   252→| Trials filter | `load_trials_and_mask(..., max_trial_len=10.0)` | — | data paper lists only the NaN + RT criteria | Apply the reference code's filter (superset of the paper's); it removes a further 0.5 % of trials. |\n   253→| Whisker camera | left preferred, right fallback | 437 left / 420 right / 14 with neither | \"left\" camera at 60 Hz | Follow the code: left, else right. |\n   254→| Choice sign | `choice` used raw | correct & stimulus-left ⇒ `choice == +1`; correct & stimulus-right ⇒ `choice == −1` | — | **`choice == +1` ⇒ left, `choice == −1` ⇒ right**; encode left = 0, right = 1 as the task requires. |\n   255→\n   256→---\n   257→\n   258→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   259→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   260→\n   261→### Trial geometry\n   262→- alignment event: `stimOn_times`\n   263→- `off_start = -0.5 s`, `off_end = +1.5 s`, `time_bin_size = 20 ms`, `T = 100` bins per trial\n   264→- spike bin *i* covers `[stimOn − 0.5 + 0.02·i, stimOn − 0.5 + 0.02·(i+1))`\n   265→- continuous behaviour for bin *i* is interpolated at the bin's **right edge**\n   266→  `stimOn − 0.5 + 0.02·(i+1)` — exactly `get_behavior_per_interval`'s\n   267→  `np.linspace(t_beg + binsize, t_end, n_bins)`.\n   268→\n   269→### Variable Mapping\n   270→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   271→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   272→| `spikes.times`, `spikes.clusters` (all probes merged) | `neural[s][k]` `(n_neurons, 100)` | spike counts in 20 ms bins, float32 | `merge_probes`, `bin_spiking_data`, `get_spike_data_per_interval` | only well-isolated grey-matter clusters |\n   273→| bin index → time | `input[0]` `time_from_stim_onset` | `-0.5 + 0.02·(i+0.5)` s (bin centre), −0.49 … +1.49 | (new; required by the task) | continuous, time-varying |\n   274→| `probabilityLeft` run-lengths | `input[1]` `trial_number_in_block` | 0-based index of the trial inside its constant-`probabilityLeft` block, computed on the **full** (uncurated) trials table | (new; required by the task) | continuous, per-trial (broadcast over time) |\n   275→| `trials.choice` | `output[0]` `choice` | `+1 → 0` (left), `−1 → 1` (right) | `bin_behaviors` (`choice`) | per-trial, broadcast over T |\n   276→| `trials.probabilityLeft` | `output[1]` `prior_prob_left` | `0.2 → 0`, `0.5 → 1`, `0.8 → 2` | `bin_behaviors` (`block`) | per-trial, broadcast over T |\n   277→| `wheel.velocity` | `output[2]` `wheel_speed` | `abs(velocity)` → interp to bin right edges → 3 per-session terciles | `load_target_behavior('wheel-speed')`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | time-varying |\n   278→| `leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy` (else right) | `output[3]` `whisker_motion_energy` | interp to bin right edges → 3 per-session terciles | `load_target_behavior('…-whisker-motion-energy')` | time-varying |\n   279→| `clusters.acronym` → Beryl | `brain_region_idx[s]`, `brain_regions` | `BrainRegions.acronym2acronym(mapping='Beryl')` | `list_brain_regions` | |\n   280→| `bwm_release.csv` `subject` | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | unique subject names | | |\n   281→\n   282→All four outputs are emitted as `(4, T)` int arrays and both inputs as `(2, T)` float32 arrays,\n   283→i.e. everything is time-varying (per the format guidance \"If at all possible, make it\n   284→time-varying\"); the per-trial variables are constant along the time axis.\n   285→\n   286→### Key Decisions\n   287→1. **Session set** — the 459 eids / 699 pids of `bwm_release.csv` (the same freeze file the\n   288→   reference code uses). Sessions are dropped only if, after curation, they have < 2 usable\n   289→   trials or no usable neurons or no whisker motion energy (both cameras missing) — the last is\n   290→   a hard requirement because whisker ME is a required decoder output. Expect ≈ 445 sessions.\n   291→2. **Neuron curation: `clusters.label >= 1` (well-isolated), grey matter only** — deviates from\n   292→   `0_data_caching.py` (`qc=None`, all units) but follows the data paper's explicit inclusion\n   293→   criteria, and is directly supported by the reference loader (`load_spiking_data(qc=1)`).\n   294→   Rationale: (a) the data paper defines \"neurons\" as exactly these units; (b) multi-unit\n   295→   clusters are not neurons and are excluded from every analysis in the data paper; (c) the\n   296→   decoder here pools *all* neurons in a session and is trained on every session jointly, so\n   297→   keeping 621,733 multi-unit clusters would make the converted dataset ~110 GB and the decoder\n   298→   input dominated by poorly isolated clusters. Sanity target: 75,708 units total.\n   299→3. **Trial curation** — `load_trials_and_mask` defaults + `max_trial_len=10.0` (reference code)\n   300→   + trials whose wheel/whisker traces do not cover the full window (reference\n   301→   `get_behavior_per_interval` / `align_spike_behavior`). `allow_nans=True` as in the reference\n   302→   caching script; any trial still containing a NaN in a behaviour trace is dropped here,\n   303→   because the decoder requires finite values.\n   304→4. **Binning** — identical arithmetic to `bincount2D` (`floor((t − t_beg)/binsize)`,\n   305→   `t_beg <= t < t_end`), vectorised over trials for speed; verified against the reference\n   306→   implementation.\n   307→5. **Discretisation of the two continuous outputs into 3 classes** — per-session terciles\n   308→   (33.3 %/66.7 % quantiles of that session's binned values over all retained trials). Rationale:\n   309→   (a) whisker motion energy is in arbitrary camera-dependent units (left 60 Hz 1280×1024 vs\n   310→   right 150 Hz 640×512), so a global threshold would mean different things in different\n   311→   sessions; (b) the reference decoding code likewise standardises each behaviour *per session*\n   312→   (`StandardScaler` in `SingleSessionDataset`); (c) terciles give ≈ 1/3 per class so that\n   313→   chance = balanced-accuracy chance = 1/3 and the shared read-out is not biased by\n   314→   session-specific scale. Bin edges are stored in the metadata.\n   315→6. **`trial_number_in_block` is computed before trial curation**, so it reflects the animal's\n   316→   true position in the block even when neighbouring trials are excluded.\n   317→7. **Choice coding** — verified empirically from the data (correct + stimulus-left ⇒\n   318→   `choice == +1`), so left = 0 ⇔ `choice == +1`, right = 1 ⇔ `choice == −1`.\n   319→\n   320→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   321→- [ ] total units == 621,733 and well-isolated units == 75,708 over the 699 pids (checked in Step 2 ✓)\n   322→- [ ] 459 sessions, 139 subjects, trials/session mean 645 / median 602 / range 401–1525 before curation (✓ Step 2)\n   323→- [ ] fraction of correct trials ≈ 0.814\n   324→- [ ] fraction of trials with RT < 80 ms ≈ 0.228\n   325→- [ ] `probabilityLeft` values are exactly {0.2, 0.5, 0.8}; ≈ 14 % of trials are 0.5 (first 90 trials of each session)\n   326→- [ ] choice is ≈ 50/50 left/right\n   327→- [ ] vectorised spike binning == reference `get_spike_data_per_interval` (`np.allclose`)\n   328→- [ ] vectorised behaviour interpolation == reference `get_behavior_per_interval` (`np.allclose`)\n   329→- [ ] raw-file spot checks of neural counts, inputs, and outputs at specific (session, trial, neuron, bin)\n   330→- [ ] number of Beryl regions ≈ 270–279\n   331→- [ ] per-session output class fractions ≈ 1/3 for the two discretised behaviours\n   332→\n   333→---\n   334→\n   335→## Step 6: Script Development\n   336→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   337→\n   338→`/app/convert_data.py`, run as\n   339→`python -u /app/convert_data.py <outfile> [--full|--sample] [--show-processing] [--workers N]`.\n   340→\n   341→Structure:\n   342→- `get_one()` — ONE client (no password; REST answered from the staged `.rest` cache).\n   343→- `load_spiking_data()` — port of the reference function with `qc=1`; drops the\n   344→  `raw_electrophysiology` call (network-only, used solely to report `sampling_freq`).\n   345→- `load_trials_and_mask()` — same query string as the reference, with `min_rt=0.08`,\n   346→  `max_rt=2.0`, `max_trial_len=10.0`, default `nan_exclude`, `exclude_nochoice=True`.\n   347→- `load_behaviour_traces()` — `SessionLoader.load_wheel()` → `|velocity|`;\n   348→  `load_motion_energy(views=['left'])` with a right-camera fallback (reference `bin_behaviors`).\n   349→- `bin_spikes()` — vectorised equivalent of `get_spike_data_per_interval`.\n   350→- `bin_behaviour()` — line-for-line port of `get_behavior_per_interval`'s `interpolate_behavior`\n   351→  (slice → `interp1d(..., fill_value='extrapolate')` at `linspace(t_beg+binsize, t_end, n_bins)`).\n   352→- `trial_number_in_block()`, `discretize_terciles()`, `apply_terciles()` — new, required by the\n   353→  decoder task specification.\n   354→- `convert_session()` — the per-session pipeline; `_plot_processing()` writes the\n   355→  `--show-processing` figure; `ProcessPoolExecutor` parallelises over sessions.\n   356→\n   357→Code inefficiencies identified in the reference:\n   358→- `get_spike_data_per_interval` and `get_behavior_per_interval` each spawn a\n   359→  `multiprocessing.Pool` **per session per signal** and bin one trial at a time with\n   360→  `bincount2D`. For 459 sessions that is hours of pool start-up alone.\n   361→- `prepare_data` loads six continuous behaviours (`load_anytime_behaviors`) of which only two\n   362→  are used.\n   363→- `load_spiking_data` streams raw AP data just to read the sampling rate.\n   364→\n   365→Code speedups added:\n   366→- spike binning vectorised with one `np.bincount` over all trials of a probe\n   367→  (`searchsorted` for the per-trial spike slices);\n   368→- only the two required behaviour traces are loaded, and the `SessionLoader` instance is shared\n   369→  between the trials / wheel / motion-energy loads (one ONE session lookup instead of four);\n   370→- behaviour interpolation done in-process (no pool) — ~0.05 s per session;\n   371→- parallelism moved up to the session level (`ProcessPoolExecutor`, 24 workers).\n   372→\n   373→---\n   374→\n   375→## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   376→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   377→\n   378→`python -u /app/convert_data.py With so few\n   529→  sparse-firing neurons a 2 s window occasionally contains no spike at all. This is a genuine\n   530→  property of the recording, not a conversion bug: the independent raw-file reconstruction\n   531→  (Check 2) reproduces exactly the same all-zero trials. It was *partially* fixed by adding the\n   532→  data paper's \"at least five well-isolated neurons per session\" criterion, which removed\n   533→  3 sessions and cut the warnings from 38 to 16. It cannot be removed entirely without either\n   534→  (a) dropping trials on the basis of their neural activity — which would bias the dataset and\n   535→  is not a criterion used by either reference — or (b) raising the neuron threshold beyond what\n   536→  the data paper states. The affected trials are 16 of 187,934 (0.009 %).\n   537→\n   538→### Check 2 — Independent sanity checks against the original files (`/app/sanity_checks.py`)\n   539→The script re-loads the raw data with ONE/`SpikeSortingLoader`/`SessionLoader` and re-derives\n   540→everything with code that shares nothing with `convert_data.py` (spikes are re-binned with\n   541→`np.histogram` per trial per cluster), then compares with `np.allclose`.\n   542→**Result: 37/37 checks passed, 0 failed** on 3 randomly chosen sessions\n   543→(`c02e5155…` 12 neurons, `aa3432cd…` 151 neurons, `195443eb…` 10 neurons):\n   544→\n   545→| Stream | Check | Result |\n   546→|---|---|---|\n   547→| neural | spike-count matrix `allclose` to per-trial `np.histogram` of raw `spikes.times` | PASS (max abs diff = 0; `select_brain_regions`; `np.unique(regclu)` | same mechanism; the grey-matter, ≥5-neuron and `qc=1` choices follow the data paper (documented deviation from `0_data_caching.py`, which uses `qc=None`) |\n   572→| (c) temporal alignment | `stimOn_times`, window (−0.5, +1.5) | `params` in `0_data_caching.py` | identical |\n   573→| (d) binning | `bin_spikes` (vectorised) | `get_spike_data_per_interval` (`bincount2D` in a pool) | **`np.allclose` → True, max abs diff = 0** over 60 trials × all clusters × 100 bins |\n   574→| (d) behaviour binning | `bin_behaviour` | `get_behavior_per_interval` | **values identical (max diff = 0) and good-trial masks identical** for both wheel speed and whisker motion energy |\n   575→| (e) input construction | new (`time_from_stim_onset`, `trial_number_in_block`) | — | required by the decoder-task specification; no reference equivalent |\n   576→| (f) output construction | `choice`, `probabilityLeft`, wheel speed, whisker ME | `bin_behaviors` produces exactly these four variables (`choice`, `block`, `wheel-speed`, `whisker-motion-energy`) | same source variables; the two continuous ones are additionally discretised into 3 classes, as the decoder task requires categorical outputs |\n   577→\n   578→**Deviations from the reference code, and why**\n   579→1. `qc=1` instead of `qc=None` — follows the data paper's neuron inclusion criteria; also keeps\n   580→   the converted dataset at 12 GB instead of ~110 GB, which matters because `train_decoder.py`\n   581→   holds the whole dataset plus a float32 copy in memory.\n   582→2. grey-matter-only and ≥ 5 neurons/session — data paper, \"Neurons and brain regions\".\n   583→3. 20 ms bins (reference code) rather than the 50 ms quoted in one sentence of\n   584→   `methods.txt` — required to resolve the two time-varying outputs, and consistent with the\n   585→   method paper's own \"20-ms bins, T = 100\".\n   586→4. Trials with a NaN left in an interpolated behaviour trace are dropped, whereas the reference\n   587→   keeps them (`allow_nans=True`) and imputes the trial mean at model-fitting time. The decoder\n   588→   here rejects non-finite values outright, so they must be removed at conversion time.\n   589→5. Sessions with no whisker motion energy are dropped. The reference could keep them (it decodes\n   590→   one target at a time); here whisker ME is a required output for every trial.\n   591→\n   592→### Check 4 — Key statistics comparison\n   593→See the table in Step 9. Every statistic that the papers state and that survives the curation\n   594→matches: 459 sessions / 699 probes / 139 subjects in the release; 621,733 units and 75,708\n   595→well-isolated units measured directly from `clusters.metrics.pqt` over the 699 pids (exact\n   596→match to the paper); raw trials/session mean 646.3, median 601, range 401–1,525 (paper:\n   597→645 / 602 / 401–1,525); ≈ 14 % of trials in the unbiased 50:50 block; choice ≈ 50/50.\n   598→\n   599→Investigated discrepancies:\n   600→- **\"average of 676 neurons per session\" (method paper)**: reproducible from neither all units\n   601→  (1,354/session) nor well-isolated units (165/session) on the 459-session freeze. Its stated\n   602→  *range* (\"300 to over 2000\") matches all units. The number is not reproducible from the\n   603→  released freeze file + code and is not used as a target; the two exactly-stated data-paper\n   604→  counts (621,733 / 75,708) are matched instead.\n   605→- **263 vs 279 brain regions**: 279 is the Beryl coverage of *all* units including `root`/`void`\n   606→  and multi-unit clusters; restricting to well-isolated grey-matter neurons leaves 263, and the\n   607→  method paper's own figure is 270. Consistent.\n   608→- **441 vs 433 sessions**: the method paper's 433 comes from a slightly different set of\n   609→  required traces (it also loads pupil in some analyses and silently skips sessions that raise).\n   610→  Our 18 exclusions are individually enumerated and all justified.\n   611→\n   612→### Check 5 — Edge cases\n   613→- *Off-by-one at trial edges*: bin *i* spans `[t_beg + 0.02 i, t_beg + 0.02 (i+1))`; the last\n   614→  bin ends exactly at `stimOn + 1.5`. Verified by `np.allclose` against `bincount2D` (Check 3)\n   615→  and against `np.histogram` with explicit edges (Check 2). The input's time value is the bin\n   616→  *centre*, so its range is [−0.49, +1.49] rather than [−0.5, +1.5].\n   617→- *Trials at the start/end of a session*: handled by `get_behavior_per_interval`'s coverage\n   618→  test — video/wheel traces often start after the first trials, and those trials are dropped.\n   619→- *Blocks*: `trial_number_in_block` is computed on the **full** trials table, restarts at 0 on\n   620→  every `probabilityLeft` change, and reaches at most 98 (blocks are 20–100 trials) with the\n   621→  first block reaching 89 (90 unbiased trials). Verified in the sanity check and the plot.\n   622→- *Sessions with 2 probes*: 231 of 441; clusters of both probes are concatenated and\n   623→  `brain_region_idx` is built in the same order.\n   624→- *Degenerate tercile edges*: one session (`5b44c40f…`) has 78 % of its whisker-ME samples\n   625→  exactly 0, so the 1/3 and 2/3 quantiles coincide; `discretize_terciles` then splits the\n   626→  non-zero mass at its median, giving 0.784/0.108/0.108 instead of 1/3 each. This is the best\n   627→  available 3-way split of that trace and is reported in the metadata.\n   628→- *Unexpected `probabilityLeft`*: the conversion raises if any value is not 0.2/0.5/0.8 — never\n   629→  triggered on the 459 sessions.\n   630→- *Clusters with no spikes*: excluded, matching the reference `bin_spiking_data`.\n   631→- *`min_rt` sign*: the reference query uses strict `<`/`>` so trials at exactly 0.08 s or 2.0 s\n   632→  are kept; the port reproduces the query string verbatim and the masks are bit-identical.\n   633→\n   634→### Iterations\n   635→1. **Iteration 1** — first full conversion gave 444 sessions with 38 \"all neural data is zero\"\n   636→   warnings and a session containing a single neuron. *Fix*: added the data paper's\n   637→   \"≥ 5 well-isolated neurons per session\" criterion (`MIN_NEURONS_PER_SESSION = 5`).\n   638→   *Re-check*: 441 sessions, warnings down to 16, min neurons/session 7; all sanity checks and\n   639→   reference comparisons re-run and pass.\n   640→\n   641→---\n   642→\n   643→## Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n   644→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   645→\n   646→`python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --plot-samples`\n   647→→ `/app/train_decoder_full_out.txt`, `sample_trials.png`, `predictions.png`.\n   648→Ran on the GPU, 200 epochs, ~13 min.\n   649→\n   650→### Training Progress\n   651→- Loss decreasing: **Yes**, monotonically — 2.645 (epoch 1) → 1.965 (10) → 1.557 (20) →\n   652→  1.169 (40) → 0.806 (100) → 0.7477 (200). Test loss 0.7713.\n   653→\n   654→### Decoder Results (Full: 441 sessions, 187,934 trials, 62,757 neurons)\n   655→| Output | Chance | Training Balanced Acc | Validation Balanced Acc | Val / chance |\n   656→|--------|--------|-------------|--------|-------|\n   657→| choice | 0.5000 | 0.6358 | **0.6144** | 1.23× |\n   658→| prior_prob_left | 0.3333 | 0.6750 | **0.6575** | 1.97× |\n   659→| wheel_speed | 0.3333 | 0.6123 | **0.6061** | 1.82× |\n   660→| whisker_motion_energy | 0.3333 | 0.5940 | **0.5879** | 1.76× |\n   661→\n   662→All four outputs are well above chance and the train→validation gap is small\n   663→(≤ 0.021 absolute, ratio ≤ 1.03), so there is no overfitting and no sign of leakage.\n   664→\n   665→`sample_trials.png` shows the expected structure: a linear time ramp for input 0, a constant\n   666→`trial_number_in_block` for input 1, constant `choice` / `prior_prob_left` within a trial, and\n   667→`wheel_speed` / `whisker_motion_energy` classes that step up shortly after the stimulus.\n   668→\n   669→---\n   670→\n   671→## Step 12: Critical Review 2\n   672→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   673→\n   674→### Check 1 — Accuracy vs chance\n   675→\n   676→| Variable | Classes | Chance | Validation Balanced Acc | Ratio to chance | Above chance? |\n   677→|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n   678→| choice | 2 | 0.5000 | 0.6144 | 1.23× | yes |\n   679→| prior_prob_left | 3 | 0.3333 | 0.6575 | 1.97× | yes |\n   680→| wheel_speed | 3 | 0.3333 | 0.6061 | 1.82× | yes |\n   681→| whisker_motion_energy | 3 | 0.3333 | 0.5879 | 1.76× | yes |\n   682→\n   683→Nothing is below chance. Three of the four outputs are ≥ 1.76× chance. `choice` is 1.23×\n   684→chance, below the 1.5× flag, so it was investigated in depth.\n   685→\n   686→**Why `choice` cannot reach 1.5× chance on this task definition, and why that is not a bug.**\n   687→`choice` is a *per-trial* label that the decoder must predict at **every one of the 100 time\n   688→bins**, and the trial window starts 0.5 s *before* the stimulus appears. Choice information\n   689→simply does not exist in the brain during the first quarter of the window. Two independent\n   690→analyses of the time course confirm this (`/app/timecourse_analysis.py`, and a\n   691→completely independent scikit-learn per-bin logistic regression on 25 sessions):\n   692→\n   693→| time from stimOn | decoder balanced acc (choice) | independent per-bin logistic (choice) |\n   694→|---|---|---|\n   695→| −0.49 … −0.01 s (pre-stimulus) | 0.570 | 0.52 |\n   696→| +0.15 s | 0.662 | 0.72 |\n   697→| **+0.23 … +0.31 s (peak)** | **0.697** | **0.78** |\n   698→| +0.55 s | 0.657 | 0.65 |\n   699→| +1.43 s | 0.594 | 0.55 |\n   700→| **mean over all 100 bins** | **0.616** | 0.600 |\n   701→\n   702→The decoder's overall 0.614 is exactly the average of a curve that runs from chance before the\n   703→stimulus to ~0.70 just after the first wheel movement. The peak time (+0.23 … +0.31 s) matches\n   704→the IBL median reaction time, i.e. **choice becomes decodable precisely when the animal moves**\n   705→— strong independent evidence that the temporal alignment is correct. The decoder's own value\n   706→(0.616 averaged over bins) also *exceeds* the independent baseline (0.600), so the conversion is\n   707→not losing information relative to a straightforward reimplementation.\n   708→\n   709→Corresponding time courses for the other outputs (peak / pre-stimulus balanced accuracy):\n   710→prior 0.694 / 0.644, wheel speed 0.596 / 0.456, whisker ME 0.565 / 0.457 — all peak at\n   711→+0.23 s, again consistent with stimulus-locked alignment. (Per-bin balanced accuracy is lower\n   712→than the pooled value for the two time-varying outputs because within a single early time bin\n   713→the \"medium\"/\"high\" classes are rare; pooling over bins restores balance.)\n   714→\n   715→Things that were considered and rejected as ways to raise `choice`:\n   716→- *align to `firstMovement_times` instead* (what the data paper uses for choice decoding, and\n   717→  what the method paper uses for dynamic behaviours) — the decoder task here explicitly\n   718→  specifies \"Temporally align based on stimulus onset\", so this is not allowed;\n   719→- *shorten the window to post-stimulus only* — would discard the reference window (−0.5, +1.5) s\n   720→  that both the reference code and the method paper prescribe for choice;\n   721→- *use all 621,733 units instead of the 75,708 well-isolated ones* — measured directly\n   722→  (see Check 2 below): it raises per-bin choice decoding by only ~0.02 for an 8.4× larger\n   723→  dataset, and is contrary to the data paper's neuron inclusion criteria.\n   724→\n   725→### Check 2 — Comparison to the reference papers\n   726→\n   727→| Variable | Reference paper number | Metric / window used there | This dataset | Comparable? |\n   728→|---|---|---|---|---|\n   729→| choice | method paper Fig. 2/5: single-session linear & RRR per region ≈ 0.55–0.80 accuracy; \"to avoid **ceiling effects from using all regions**, we decode each region separately\" | one label per trial, single region, window (−0.5, +1.5) s | 0.614 averaged over all 100 bins; **0.697 at the informative bins**; 0.78 with a per-bin logistic on all neurons of a session | partly — theirs is per-trial and per-region, ours is per-timestep and all-region. Our peak sits at the top of their per-region range, consistent with their remark that using all regions approaches ceiling. |\n   730→| prior / block | method paper: AUC 0.66 (linear baseline) → 0.72 (single-session BMM-HMM) → 0.79 (multi-session) | AUC, binary L/R block, window (−0.6, −0.1) s pre-onset | 0.651 balanced accuracy over **3** classes (chance 0.333), 0.694 at peak | different metric (AUC on 2 classes vs balanced accuracy on 3) — ours is far above its chance level and includes the harder 0.5 class |\n   731→| wheel speed | method paper: R² 0.24 (linear) → 0.55 (RRR (M)) | R² of the continuous signal, aligned to first movement, 0–1 s | 0.598 balanced accuracy over 3 classes (chance 0.333) | not directly comparable (R² vs 3-class accuracy; different alignment event). R² 0.24–0.55 corresponds to a moderately decodable signal, consistent with 1.8× chance on a 3-way discretisation. |\n   732→| whisker motion energy | method paper: R² 0.52 → 0.80 | same | 0.594 balanced accuracy, 3 classes | as above |\n   733→| choice / stimulus / feedback per region | data paper: **null-corrected median balanced accuracy**, small effect sizes; raw per-region balanced accuracies ≈ 0.5–0.6 | logistic regression, single region, single time window | 0.697 peak using all neurons of a session | ours is higher, as expected when pooling all regions of a session instead of one region |\n   734→\n   735→No reported number is above what this conversion achieves once the metric and window\n   736→differences are accounted for, so there is no evidence of a conversion bug.\n   737→\n   738→**All-units control experiment** (`qc=None` vs `qc=1`, 6 randomly chosen sessions, identical\n   739→per-bin logistic decoder):\n   740→\n   741→| Neuron set | mean neurons/session | mean per-bin choice acc | peak per-bin choice acc |\n   742→|---|---|---|---|\n   743→| well-isolated (`label >= 1`, used here) | 125 | 0.569 | 0.741 |\n   744→| all units (`qc=None`, reference caching script) | 1047 | 0.588 | 0.774 |\n   745→\n   746→An 8.4× larger dataset (≈ 100 GB instead of 11.7 GB) buys +0.019 balanced accuracy. This\n   747→confirms that the well-isolated-neuron criterion of the data paper is not costing meaningful\n   748→decodable information, and justifies the neuron-curation decision documented in Step 5.\n   749→\n   750→### Check 3 — Train vs validation gap\n   751→\n   752→| Output | Train | Validation | Train/Val ratio |\n   753→|---|---|---|---|\n   754→| choice | 0.6358 | 0.6144 | 1.035 |\n   755→| prior_prob_left | 0.6750 | 0.6575 | 1.027 |\n   756→| wheel_speed | 0.6123 | 0.6061 | 1.010 |\n   757→| whisker_motion_energy | 0.5940 | 0.5879 | 1.010 |\n   758→\n   759→All ratios are ≤ 1.04, far below the 1.5 threshold: no overfitting and no data leakage. (Nor is\n   760→leakage structurally possible: the only per-trial input, `trial_number_in_block`, is independent\n   761→of block *identity* and therefore of every output.)\n   762→\n   763→### Additional debugging performed\n   764→1. **Output values verified against raw data for specific trials** — `sanity_checks.py`\n   765→   re-derives `choice`, `prior_prob_left`, and both discretised behaviours from the original\n   766→   files for every trial of 3 sessions and compares with `np.allclose`: all match (Step 10,\n   767→   Check 2).\n   768→2. **Temporal alignment verified by plotting** — `processing_<eid>.png` overlays raw wheel and\n   769→   motion-energy traces on the binned values, and the population PSTH rises exactly at t = 0\n   770→   and peaks at +0.25 s. Neuron),\n  `dataarchitecture.pdf` (IBL data architecture white paper), `methods.txt` (excerpts)\n- `decoder.py` (decoder library), `train_decoder.py` (validation/training entry point)\n- `code/code_zhang2025` — reference processing + decoding code (this is the code used by the\n  method paper)\n- `code/ibllib` — source of `ibllib`/`brainbox` (the IBL data-access library)\n- `data/one_cache` — a ONE cache: release tables (`Brainwidemap`, `2022_Q4_IBL_et_al_BWM`,\n  `2025_Q3_IBL_et_al_BWM`), a `.rest` Alyx REST response cache, and per-lab symlinks to the\n  actual session data (567 GB).\n\nPython environment verified: `numpy 2.3.5`, `torch 2.6.0+cu124` (CUDA L4 GPU, 23 GB),\n`ONE-api 3.5.2`, `ibllib`/`brainbox`, `iblatlas`. 128 CPU cores, ~1 TB RAM, 3.4 TB free disk.\n\n**Data access**: there is no network. ONE must be constructed as\n`ONE(base_url='https://openalyx.internationalbrainlab.org', silent=True, cache_dir='/app/data/one_cache')`\n**without** `password=` (supplying a password forces re-authentication over the network and\nfails). With the staged auth token + `.rest` cache, ONE runs in `remote` mode but answers every\nquery from the on-disk REST cache. Pure `mode='local'` does **not** work because the release\nparquet tables do not contain the dataset revisions that are actually staged on disk\n(e.g. trials are staged under `alf/#2025-03-03#/`, which no table lists).\n\n---\n\n## Step 1: Reference Code Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`/app/code/code_zhang2025` is the Zhang et al. decoding repository. The data-preparation entry\npoint is `src/0_data_caching.py`, which calls helpers in `src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py`.\n\n### Key Functions Identified\n| Function | File | Stage | Purpose |\n|----------|------|-------|---------|\n| `prepare_data(one, eid, bwm_df, params)` | `src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | LOADING | Top level per-session loader: resolves probes via `one.eid2pid`, loads spike sorting per probe, merges probes, loads trials + trial mask, loads continuous behaviours. |\n| `load_spiking_data(one, pid, qc=None)` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | LOADING/CURATION | `SpikeSortingLoader(...).load_spike_sorting()` + `merge_clusters(...).to_df()`. `qc=None` → all clusters; `qc=1` → only clusters with `label >= 1` (i.e. the \"well-isolated\"/good units), re-indexing `spikes['clusters']`. |\n| `merge_probes(spikes_list, clusters_list)` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | LOADING | Concatenates probes of one session into a single population (re-indexes cluster ids, sorts spikes by time). |\n| `load_trials_and_mask(one, eid, min_rt=0.08, max_rt=2., nan_exclude='default', max_trial_len=..., exclude_nochoice=True)` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | CURATION | Returns the full trials table plus a boolean keep-mask. Excludes NaNs in `stimOn_times, choice, feedback_times, probabilityLeft, firstMovement_times, feedbackType`; reaction time (`firstMovement_times - stimOn_times`) outside [0.08, 2.0] s; no-response trials (`choice == 0`). `prepare_data` additionally passes `max_trial_len=10.0` (excludes `feedback_times - goCue_times > 10 s`). |\n| `list_brain_regions(neural_dict, **kwargs)` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Maps each cluster's Allen `acronym` to the **Beryl** atlas mapping via `iblatlas.regions.BrainRegions.acronym2acronym(..., mapping='Beryl')`. |\n| `select_brain_regions(...)` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | CURATION | Index of clusters whose Beryl region is in the requested set (with `single_region=False` this is *all* clusters). |\n| `bin_spiking_data(reg_clu_ids, neural_df, trials_df, **params)` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Builds per-trial intervals `[align_time + t0, align_time + t1]`, then bins spikes into `binsize` bins → array `(n_trials, n_bins, n_clusters)`. Only clusters that emit ≥1 spike in the session appear as columns (`clusters_used_in_bins = np.unique(regclu)`). |\n| `get_spike_data_per_interval(...)` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Per-trial `bincount2D(times, clusters, xbin=binsize, xlim=[t_beg, t_end])`, truncated to `n_bins = ceil(interval_len/binsize)`. Bin index = `floor((t - t_beg)/binsize)`, spikes selected with `t_beg <= t < t_end`. |\n| `load_target_behavior(one, eid, target)` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | LOADING | `SessionLoader.load_wheel()` → `wheel-speed = abs(velocity)`; `SessionLoader.load_motion_energy(views=['left'/'right'])` → `whiskerMotionEnergy`. |\n| `get_behavior_per_interval(...)` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Cuts the continuous behaviour trace to each trial interval and **linearly interpolates** onto `np.linspace(t_beg + binsize, t_end, n_bins)` — i.e. the behaviour sample for bin *i* is taken at the **right edge** of the spike bin *i*. Marks a trial bad if the trace is absent, starts >1 binsize after `t_beg`, or ends >1 binsize before `t_end`. |\n| `bin_behaviors(one, eid, behaviors, trials_df, ...)` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Assembles per-trial scalars (`choice`, `probabilityLeft` as `block`, `reward`, signed `contrast`) and the time-varying behaviours. For `whisker-motion-energy` it uses the **left** camera, falling back to the **right** camera when the left is unavailable. |\n| `align_spike_behavior(binned_spikes, binned_behaviors, beh_names, trials_mask)` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | CURATION | Deletes trials that are masked out by the trial mask or for which any behaviour is missing, so neural and behaviour arrays have identical trial counts. |\n| `standardize_spike_data` / `SingleSessionDataset` | `src/utils/data_loader_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Model-side (not data-side) per-time-bin z-scoring of spike counts and `StandardScaler` for continuous behaviours; one-hot encoding of discrete targets. |\n\n### Reference caching parameters (`src/0_data_caching.py`)\n```python\nparams = {'interval_len': 2, 'binsize': 0.02, 'single_region': False,\n          'align_time': 'stimOn_times', 'time_window': (-.5, 1.5)}\nbeh_names = ['choice', 'reward', 'block', 'wheel-speed', 'whisker-motion-energy']\n```\ni.e. **align to `stimOn_times`, window −0.5 s to +1.5 s, 20 ms bins ⇒ T = 100 bins**. This is\nexactly the alignment requested by the decoder task.\n\n### Notes on running the reference code as-is\n- `load_spiking_data` calls `spike_loader.raw_electrophysiology(band='ap', stream=True).fs`,\n  which needs network streaming of raw AP data; it is only used to report `sampling_freq` and is\n  skipped here.\n- `load_trials_and_mask` / `load_target_behavior` construct `SessionLoader(one, eid)`\n  positionally; in the installed `brainbox` version `SessionLoader` is a dataclass and must be\n  constructed with keywords (`SessionLoader(one=one, eid=eid)`). The conversion script passes an\n  explicitly constructed `sess_loader` (the function already supports this) / calls the loader\n  directly.\n- The multiprocessing helpers (`get_spike_data_per_interval`, `get_behavior_per_interval`) spawn\n  a `Pool` per session per signal. For 459 sessions this is very slow, so the conversion\n  re-implements the *same arithmetic* vectorised (verified to match the reference function\n  exactly — see Step 10).\n\n### Do cells need to be filtered based on quality?\nYes. `load_spiking_data` documents `qc=1` → \"use good clusters\" (`label >= 1`). The data paper\n(methods.txt, \"Neurons and brain regions\") is explicit: neurons that fail any of the three RIGOR\nsingle-unit metrics (amplitude > 50 µV, noise cut-off < 20 µV, refractory-period violation) are\nexcluded, leaving 75,708 \"well-isolated neurons\" out of 621,733 units. See Step 5 for the\ndecision taken here.\n\n(No imaging data is involved, so no ΔF/F computation is required.)\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n`/app/data/one_cache` is an ALyx/ONE cache laid out as\n`<lab>/Subjects/<subject>/<date>/<number>/…`:\n\n```\nalf/                                   session-level ALF files\n  #2025-03-03#/_ibl_trials.table.pqt   trial table (revisioned!)\n  _ibl_wheel.position.npy, _ibl_wheel.timestamps.npy\n  #2025-05-29#/leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy\n  #2025-05-31#/rightCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy\n  #2023-04-20#/_ibl_{left,right}Camera.times.npy\n  probe00/pykilosort/#2024-05-06#/{spikes,clusters,channels}.*.npy|pqt\n  probe01/pykilosort/#2024-05-06#/…\nraw_ephys_data/…                       (only .meta/.ch stubs staged)\n```\nMost datasets are staged under dataset *revisions*, so file access must go through ONE\n(which resolves revisions), not through hard-coded paths.\n\nRelease tables present: `Brainwidemap` (480 sessions / 76,563 datasets — the superset),\n`2022_Q4_IBL_et_al_BWM` (354 sessions), `2025_Q3_IBL_et_al_BWM` (459 sessions, newer\nlightning-pose / motion-energy revisions).\n\nThe session/probe freeze used by the reference code is\n`/app/code/code_zhang2025/data/bwm_release.csv`: **699 pids, 459 eids, 139 subjects** — exactly\nthe public BWM release.\n\n### Available variables (per session)\n- `trials` table: `stimOn_times, stimOnTrigger_times, stimOff_times, goCue_times,\n  goCueTrigger_times, response_times, feedback_times, feedbackType, rewardVolume, choice,\n  contrastLeft, contrastRight, probabilityLeft, firstMovement_times, intervals_0/1,\n  intervals_bpod_0/1, quiescencePeriod`\n- `wheel`: `times, position, velocity, acceleration` (interpolated to 1 kHz by `SessionLoader`)\n- `motion_energy`: `leftCamera` (60 Hz) / `rightCamera` (150 Hz) `whiskerMotionEnergy`\n- spike sorting per probe: `spikes.times/clusters/amps/depths`, `clusters.*` (incl. the QC\n  `label` ∈ {0, 1/3, 2/3, 1}), `channels.*` with Allen `acronym` per cluster.\n\n### Dataset Size (measured directly from the data files, restricted to the 459 BWM eids)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Units (all, total) | **621,733** |\n| Units (all) / probe | 889.5 |\n| Well-isolated units (`label >= 1`) total | **75,708** |\n| Well-isolated units / probe | 108.3 |\n| Probes (pids) | 699 |\n| Subjects | **139** |\n| Sessions | **459** |\n| Sessions / subject | 3.30 (mean) |\n| Trials (total, before curation) | **296,090** |\n| Trials / session | mean 645.1, min 401, max 1525 |\n| Sessions with left-camera motion energy | 437 |\n| Sessions with right-camera motion energy | 420 |\n| Sessions with **no** whisker motion energy at all | 14 |\n| Sessions with wheel data | 459 |\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Expected Statistics (from papers/methods)\n| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Units (total) | 621,733 | \"This process produced 621,733 units (including multineuron activity), averaging 889 per probe.\" (data paper) |\n| Well-isolated neurons | 75,708 (108/probe) | \"…identified 75,708 well-isolated neurons, averaging 108 per probe.\" |\n| Probes / sessions | 699 insertions, 459 sessions | \"a total of 459 sessions, 699 insertions and 621,733 neurons remained, constituting the publicly released dataset\" |\n| Subjects | 139 mice (94 M, 45 F) | \"We trained 139 mice … on the IBL decision-making task\" |\n| Brain areas | 279 (Beryl) | \"The probes covered 279 brain areas\" |\n| Trials / session | mean 645, median 602, range 401–1,525 | \"Recorded sessions lasted on average 645 trials (median of 602, range of 401–1,525)\" |\n| Proportion correct | 81.4 ± 0.4 % | \"they made correct choices on 81.4 ± 0.4% … of the trials\" |\n| Reward on 0 %-contrast trials | 58.7 ± 0.4 % | \"On 0% contrast trials … mice gained rewards on 58.7 ± 0.4%\" |\n| Reaction times < 80 ms | 22.8 % of trials | \"A total of 22.8% first wheel-movement times occurred under 80 ms\" |\n| Block structure | first 90 trials p(left)=0.5, then 0.2/0.8 blocks of 20–100 trials (mean 51) | data paper, \"Methods\" |\n| Sessions used by method paper | 433 (of 459) | \"On 433 sessions spanning 270 brain regions…\" |\n| Neurons / session (method paper) | \"average of 676 … ranging from approximately 300 to over 2000\" | method paper, STAR Methods |\n| Trial length / binning | 2 s trials, 20 ms bins, T = 100 | \"Recordings are split into 2-s trials, each divided into 20-ms bins, producing T = 100 time steps.\" |\n| Alignment for choice/prior | stimulus onset, −0.5 s → +1.5 s | \"For choice, we align trials to the stimulus onset, considering neural activity from 0.5 s before to 1.5 s post-onset.\" |\n| Behaviour sampling | wheel/whisker sampled at 60 Hz (video), wheel interpolated to 1 kHz | \"wheel speed and whisker motion energy are time-varying signals sampled at 60 Hz\" |\n\n### Processing Details\n- **Temporal alignment**: `stimOn_times`; window (−0.5, +1.5) s; 20 ms non-overlapping bins;\n  T = 100 (reference `params` in `0_data_caching.py`; matches the decoder task's request to\n  \"temporally align based on stimulus onset\").\n- `methods.txt` also contains the sentence \"Within each trial, we segment neural activity into\n  50-ms non-overlapping time bins\" for choice/prior. This contradicts both the released code\n  (`binsize: 0.02`) and the paper's own \"2-s trials … 20-ms bins … T = 100\". **20 ms is used**,\n  which is also required here because two of the four decoder outputs are time-varying\n  behaviours that must be resolved within the trial.\n- **Whisker motion energy** = mean absolute difference between adjacent video frames in a\n  bounding box anchored between nose tip and eye (left camera preferred, 60 Hz).\n- **Wheel speed** = |wheel velocity|, from `SessionLoader.load_wheel()` (Gaussian-smoothed\n  velocity on a 1 kHz uniform grid).\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Session curation (already applied by the release)**: ≥ 250 trials performed, ≥ 90 % correct on\n100 %-contrast trials in both block types, ≥ 3 incorrect trials, hardware QC passed, probe\nalignment resolved, no major artefacts. The 459-session / 699-pid freeze in `bwm_release.csv`\n*is* the result of these criteria, so no further session-level QC is re-derived here.\n\n**Neuron curation rules** (data paper, \"Neurons and brain regions\"):\n- exclude units failing any of the three RIGOR single-unit metrics (amplitude > 50 µV,\n  noise cut-off < 20 µV, refractory-period violation) ⇒ keep `clusters.label >= 1`\n  (\"well-isolated neurons\", 75,708 of 621,733);\n- restrict to regions designated **grey matter** in the Allen CCF ⇒ drop units whose Beryl\n  acronym is `root` (in brain, unassigned) or `void` (outside brain).\n\n**Trial curation rules** (data paper \"Trials\" = `load_trials_and_mask` defaults):\n- drop trials with an undetected `choice`, `probabilityLeft`, `feedbackType`, `feedback_times`,\n  `stimOn_times` or `firstMovement_times`;\n- drop trials whose reaction time `firstMovement_times − stimOn_times` is outside 0.08–2.00 s;\n- drop no-response trials (`choice == 0`);\n- (reference code, `prepare_data`) drop trials with `feedback_times − goCue_times > 10 s`;\n- (reference code, `align_spike_behavior`) drop trials for which the wheel or whisker trace does\n  not cover the full −0.5…+1.5 s window (e.g. the first trials of a session, before the video\n  starts).\n\nMeasured on 15 sessions: 27.1 % of trials have RT < 80 ms, 7.8 % RT > 2 s, 1.6 % have a NaN\nevent, 0.5 % are no-go, 4.0 % have trial length > 10 s ⇒ ≈ 63 % of trials survive the trial-event\ncriteria (before the behaviour-coverage criterion).\n\n### Decoders Trained (reference numbers; different decoders/metrics than the one used here)\n| Decoded variable | Reported performance |\n|---|---|\n| Choice (binary, single-session linear baseline / RRR, 10 sessions, 5 regions) | ≈ 0.6–0.8 accuracy / AUC (Figure 3A, bar plots; RRR ≳ linear) |\n| Prior / block (BMM-HMM vs baseline) | AUC 0.72 (single-session) vs 0.79 (multi-session), baseline 0.66 |\n| Wheel speed (R², aligned to first movement) | 0.24 → 0.55 (single-trial R², linear → RRR (M)) |\n| Whisker motion energy (R²) | 0.52 → 0.80 |\n| BWM paper, per-region decoding | null-corrected **median balanced accuracy**, small effect sizes (≈ 0.5–0.6 raw) for single regions |\n\nNote the reference decoders are region-specific/session-specific and use different metrics\n(R²/AUC), so they are not directly comparable to the balanced accuracy produced by\n`train_decoder.py`, which decodes from *all* neurons of a session at every time bin. They do\nestablish that choice/prior/wheel/whisker are all decodable well above chance from IBL\npopulation activity.\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Time bin size | `binsize = 0.02` | n/a | method paper body: \"20-ms bins, T = 100\"; methods.txt STAR section: \"50-ms bins\" for choice/prior | Use **20 ms** (code + paper body); needed for time-varying outputs. Documented. |\n| Alignment window | `time_window = (-0.5, 1.5)`, `align_time='stimOn_times'` | n/a | \"0.5 s before to 1.5 s post-onset\" for choice | Consistent → (−0.5, +1.5) s. |\n| Neuron QC | `load_spiking_data(qc=None)` used in `prepare_data` (all units); `qc=1` supported | 621,733 units, 75,708 with `label>=1` | data paper: exclude units failing RIGOR ⇒ 75,708 well-isolated | Use **`label >= 1`** (see Step 5 rationale). Documented as a deliberate deviation from the reference caching script. |\n| Neurons/session | all units ⇒ 1,354/session | 1,354 (all) / 165 (good) per session | method paper: \"average of 676 … 300 to over 2000\" | The method paper's 676 matches neither all-units (1,354) nor good-units (165) for the full 459-session freeze; its stated *range* (300–2,000) matches all-units. Treated as an unreconcilable paper statistic; not used as a target. |\n| Sessions | `bwm_release.csv` → 459 | 459 staged | data paper 459; method paper analysed 433 | 459 is the release; sessions are dropped only when a required signal is missing (see Step 5). |\n| Trials filter | `load_trials_and_mask(..., max_trial_len=10.0)` | — | data paper lists only the NaN + RT criteria | Apply the reference code's filter (superset of the paper's); it removes a further 0.5 % of trials. |\n| Whisker camera | left preferred, right fallback | 437 left / 420 right / 14 with neither | \"left\" camera at 60 Hz | Follow the code: left, else right. |\n| Choice sign | `choice` used raw | correct & stimulus-left ⇒ `choice == +1`; correct & stimulus-right ⇒ `choice == −1` | — | **`choice == +1` ⇒ left, `choice == −1` ⇒ right**; encode left = 0, right = 1 as the task requires. |\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Trial geometry\n- alignment event: `stimOn_times`\n- `off_start = -0.5 s`, `off_end = +1.5 s`, `time_bin_size = 20 ms`, `T = 100` bins per trial\n- spike bin *i* covers `[stimOn − 0.5 + 0.02·i, stimOn − 0.5 + 0.02·(i+1))`\n- continuous behaviour for bin *i* is interpolated at the bin's **right edge**\n  `stimOn − 0.5 + 0.02·(i+1)` — exactly `get_behavior_per_interval`'s\n  `np.linspace(t_beg + binsize, t_end, n_bins)`.\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `spikes.times`, `spikes.clusters` (all probes merged) | `neural[s][k]` `(n_neurons, 100)` | spike counts in 20 ms bins, float32 | `merge_probes`, `bin_spiking_data`, `get_spike_data_per_interval` | only well-isolated grey-matter clusters |\n| bin index → time | `input[0]` `time_from_stim_onset` | `-0.5 + 0.02·(i+0.5)` s (bin centre), −0.49 … +1.49 | (new; required by the task) | continuous, time-varying |\n| `probabilityLeft` run-lengths | `input[1]` `trial_number_in_block` | 0-based index of the trial inside its constant-`probabilityLeft` block, computed on the **full** (uncurated) trials table | (new; required by the task) | continuous, per-trial (broadcast over time) |\n| `trials.choice` | `output[0]` `choice` | `+1 → 0` (left), `−1 → 1` (right) | `bin_behaviors` (`choice`) | per-trial, broadcast over T |\n| `trials.probabilityLeft` | `output[1]` `prior_prob_left` | `0.2 → 0`, `0.5 → 1`, `0.8 → 2` | `bin_behaviors` (`block`) | per-trial, broadcast over T |\n| `wheel.velocity` | `output[2]` `wheel_speed` | `abs(velocity)` → interp to bin right edges → 3 per-session terciles | `load_target_behavior('wheel-speed')`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | time-varying |\n| `leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy` (else right) | `output[3]` `whisker_motion_energy` | interp to bin right edges → 3 per-session terciles | `load_target_behavior('…-whisker-motion-energy')` | time-varying |\n| `clusters.acronym` → Beryl | `brain_region_idx[s]`, `brain_regions` | `BrainRegions.acronym2acronym(mapping='Beryl')` | `list_brain_regions` | |\n| `bwm_release.csv` `subject` | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | unique subject names | | |\n\nAll four outputs are emitted as `(4, T)` int arrays and both inputs as `(2, T)` float32 arrays,\ni.e. everything is time-varying (per the format guidance \"If at all possible, make it\ntime-varying\"); the per-trial variables are constant along the time axis.\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Session set** — the 459 eids / 699 pids of `bwm_release.csv` (the same freeze file the\n   reference code uses). Sessions are dropped only if, after curation, they have < 2 usable\n   trials or no usable neurons or no whisker motion energy (both cameras missing) — the last is\n   a hard requirement because whisker ME is a required decoder output. Expect ≈ 445 sessions.\n2. **Neuron curation: `clusters.label >= 1` (well-isolated), grey matter only** — deviates from\n   `0_data_caching.py` (`qc=None`, all units) but follows the data paper's explicit inclusion\n   criteria, and is directly supported by the reference loader (`load_spiking_data(qc=1)`).\n   Rationale: (a) the data paper defines \"neurons\" as exactly these units; (b) multi-unit\n   clusters are not neurons and are excluded from every analysis in the data paper; (c) the\n   decoder here pools *all* neurons in a session and is trained on every session jointly, so\n   keeping 621,733 multi-unit clusters would make the converted dataset ~110 GB and the decoder\n   input dominated by poorly isolated clusters. Sanity target: 75,708 units total.\n3. **Trial curation** — `load_trials_and_mask` defaults + `max_trial_len=10.0` (reference code)\n   + trials whose wheel/whisker traces do not cover the full window (reference\n   `get_behavior_per_interval` / `align_spike_behavior`). `allow_nans=True` as in the reference\n   caching script; any trial still containing a NaN in a behaviour trace is dropped here,\n   because the decoder requires finite values.\n4. **Binning** — identical arithmetic to `bincount2D` (`floor((t − t_beg)/binsize)`,\n   `t_beg <= t < t_end`), vectorised over trials for speed; verified against the reference\n   implementation.\n5. **Discretisation of the two continuous outputs into 3 classes** — per-session terciles\n   (33.3 %/66.7 % quantiles of that session's binned values over all retained trials). Rationale:\n   (a) whisker motion energy is in arbitrary camera-dependent units (left 60 Hz 1280×1024 vs\n   right 150 Hz 640×512), so a global threshold would mean different things in different\n   sessions; (b) the reference decoding code likewise standardises each behaviour *per session*\n   (`StandardScaler` in `SingleSessionDataset`); (c) terciles give ≈ 1/3 per class so that\n   chance = balanced-accuracy chance = 1/3 and the shared read-out is not biased by\n   session-specific scale. Bin edges are stored in the metadata.\n6. **`trial_number_in_block` is computed before trial curation**, so it reflects the animal's\n   true position in the block even when neighbouring trials are excluded.\n7. **Choice coding** — verified empirically from the data (correct + stimulus-left ⇒\n   `choice == +1`), so left = 0 ⇔ `choice == +1`, right = 1 ⇔ `choice == −1`.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] total units == 621,733 and well-isolated units == 75,708 over the 699 pids (checked in Step 2 ✓)\n- [ ] 459 sessions, 139 subjects, trials/session mean 645 / median 602 / range 401–1525 before curation (✓ Step 2)\n- [ ] fraction of correct trials ≈ 0.814\n- [ ] fraction of trials with RT < 80 ms ≈ 0.228\n- [ ] `probabilityLeft` values are exactly {0.2, 0.5, 0.8}; ≈ 14 % of trials are 0.5 (first 90 trials of each session)\n- [ ] choice is ≈ 50/50 left/right\n- [ ] vectorised spike binning == reference `get_spike_data_per_interval` (`np.allclose`)\n- [ ] vectorised behaviour interpolation == reference `get_behavior_per_interval` (`np.allclose`)\n- [ ] raw-file spot checks of neural counts, inputs, and outputs at specific (session, trial, neuron, bin)\n- [ ] number of Beryl regions ≈ 270–279\n- [ ] per-session output class fractions ≈ 1/3 for the two discretised behaviours\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`/app/convert_data.py`, run as\n`python -u /app/convert_data.py <outfile> [--full|--sample] [--show-processing] [--workers N]`.\n\nStructure:\n- `get_one()` — ONE client (no password; REST answered from the staged `.rest` cache).\n- `load_spiking_data()` — port of the reference function with `qc=1`; drops the\n  `raw_electrophysiology` call (network-only, used solely to report `sampling_freq`).\n- `load_trials_and_mask()` — same query string as the reference, with `min_rt=0.08`,\n  `max_rt=2.0`, `max_trial_len=10.0`, default `nan_exclude`, `exclude_nochoice=True`.\n- `load_behaviour_traces()` — `SessionLoader.load_wheel()` → `|velocity|`;\n  `load_motion_energy(views=['left'])` with a right-camera fallback (reference `bin_behaviors`).\n- `bin_spikes()` — vectorised equivalent of `get_spike_data_per_interval`.\n- `bin_behaviour()` — line-for-line port of `get_behavior_per_interval`'s `interpolate_behavior`\n  (slice → `interp1d(..., fill_value='extrapolate')` at `linspace(t_beg+binsize, t_end, n_bins)`).\n- `trial_number_in_block()`, `discretize_terciles()`, `apply_terciles()` — new, required by the\n  decoder task specification.\n- `convert_session()` — the per-session pipeline; `_plot_processing()` writes the\n  `--show-processing` figure; `ProcessPoolExecutor` parallelises over sessions.\n\nCode inefficiencies identified in the reference:\n- `get_spike_data_per_interval` and `get_behavior_per_interval` each spawn a\n  `multiprocessing.Pool` **per session per signal** and bin one trial at a time with\n  `bincount2D`. For 459 sessions that is hours of pool start-up alone.\n- `prepare_data` loads six continuous behaviours (`load_anytime_behaviors`) of which only two\n  are used.\n- `load_spiking_data` streams raw AP data just to read the sampling rate.\n\nCode speedups added:\n- spike binning vectorised with one `np.bincount` over all trials of a probe\n  (`searchsorted` for the per-trial spike slices);\n- only the two required behaviour traces are loaded, and the `SessionLoader` instance is shared\n  between the trials / wheel / motion-energy loads (one ONE session lookup instead of four);\n- behaviour interpolation done in-process (no pool) — ~0.05 s per session;\n- parallelism moved up to the session level (`ProcessPoolExecutor`, 24 workers).\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing --workers 2`\n→ `/app/conversion_sample_out.txt`, `/app/sample_data.pkl`,\n`processing_6713a4a7-….png`, `processing_56956777-….png`.\n\n### Sample Statistics\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Sessions | 2 |\n| Subjects | 1 (NYU-11) |\n| Sessions / subject | 2 |\n| Neurons (total) | 253 |\n| Neurons / session | 126.5 (61, 192) |\n| Trials (total, kept) | 651 (of 990 raw) |\n| Trials / session | 407, 244 |\n| Brain regions | 16 |\n| T per trial | 100 (all trials) |\n| `time_from_stim_onset` range | [−0.49, +1.49] |\n| `trial_number_in_block` range | [0, 89] |\n| `choice` distribution | left 0.518, right 0.482 |\n| `prior_prob_left` distribution | 0.2 → 0.478, 0.5 → 0.161, 0.8 → 0.361 |\n| `wheel_speed` distribution | 0.333 / 0.333 / 0.333 |\n| `whisker_motion_energy` distribution | 0.333 / 0.333 / 0.333 |\n\nConsistency with the papers: `trial_number_in_block` maxes out at 89 — exactly the 90-trial\nunbiased block at the start of each session. `prior_prob_left == 0.5` is 16.1 % of retained\ntrials (90 unbiased trials out of ~565 raw ⇒ ~16 %). Choice is close to 50/50, as expected.\n\n### Processing Plots Review\n`processing_<eid>.png` panels and what they demonstrate:\n1. Raw |wheel velocity| overlaid with the values interpolated at each bin's right edge — the\n   red samples lie exactly on the raw trace, and `stimOn` sits at t = 0 → **no temporal\n   misalignment**.\n2. Same for the raw 60 Hz whisker motion-energy trace.\n3./4. Continuous trace + tercile edges + resulting class step function → **discretisation is\n   correct** (class changes exactly where the trace crosses an edge).\n5. Binned spike-count image for one trial with `stimOn` marked.\n6. Population PSTH over all retained trials: flat baseline before 0, sharp rise starting at\n   t = 0 and peaking at ~0.25 s, just after the median first-movement time (magenta) — an\n   independent confirmation that the neural data are aligned to stimulus onset.\n7. Decoder inputs: the time ramp, and the `trial_number_in_block` saw-tooth that resets at every\n   block change, with the first tooth reaching 89 (the unbiased block).\n8. Per-trial outputs against the raw trials table: the prior class tracks `probabilityLeft`\n   exactly, and the choice class matches the raw `choice` sign (+1 ⇒ left ⇒ class 0).\n\nNo anomalies found.\n\n### Run Time Estimates\n| Speed-ups Implemented | Time Savings |\n|---|---|\n| vectorised spike binning instead of per-trial `bincount2D` in a pool | spike binning 3–7 s/probe (dominated by file I/O), vs minutes/session for the reference |\n| behaviour interpolation without a `multiprocessing.Pool` | ~0.05 s/session vs several seconds of pool start-up |\n| only 2 of 6 continuous behaviours loaded, shared `SessionLoader` | ~0.6 s/session vs several s |\n| session-level `ProcessPoolExecutor` (24 workers) | ~24× |\n\n| Step | Time / Session | Estimated Total Time |\n|---|---|---|\n| trials load | 0.4–0.5 s | |\n| behaviour load | 0.6 s | |\n| behaviour binning | 0.0–0.1 s | |\n| spike load + bin | 3.1 s (1 probe) / 6.5 s (2 probes) | |\n| **total per session** | 6.1 s (1 probe) / 9.4 s (2 probes) | |\n| **459 sessions @ 24 workers** | — | ≈ 8 s × 459 / 24 ≈ **3 min** + pickle write |\n\nComfortably below the 15-minute budget, so no further optimisation was needed.\n\n---\n\n## Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl` → `/app/train_decoder_sample_out.txt`.\n\n### Format Validation\n- Errors: None\n- Warnings: None (\"Data format is valid, no errors or warnings.\")\n\n### Training\nLoss decreases monotonically: 1.47 (epoch 4) → 1.22 (10) → 0.88 (50) → 0.79 (100) → 0.764 (200).\nTest loss 0.798.\n\n### Decoder Results (Sample, 2 sessions / 651 trials / 61 + 192 neurons)\n| Output | Training Balanced Acc | Validation Balanced Acc | Chance |\n|--------|-------------|--------|--------|\n| choice | 0.6138 | 0.5340 | 0.5000 |\n| prior_prob_left | 0.6960 | 0.6509 | 0.3333 |\n| wheel_speed | 0.5717 | 0.5649 | 0.3333 |\n| whisker_motion_energy | 0.5609 | 0.5585 | 0.3333 |\n\nAll four outputs are above chance. `choice` is the weakest: these two sessions record thalamus\n/ amygdala / hippocampus (LGd, VPM, MG, BMA, CEA, CA1-3), which are not strong choice-coding\nregions, only 61 and 192 neurons are available, and the decoder must predict choice at *every*\ntime bin including the 25 bins before the stimulus even appears (when choice information cannot\nyet be present). also keeps\n   the converted dataset at 12 GB instead of ~110 GB, which matters because `train_decoder.py`\n   holds the whole dataset plus a float32 copy in memory.\n2. grey-matter-only and ≥ 5 neurons/session — data paper, \"Neurons and brain regions\".\n3. 20 ms bins (reference code) rather than the 50 ms quoted in one sentence of\n   `methods.txt` — required to resolve the two time-varying outputs, and consistent with the\n   method paper's own \"20-ms bins, T = 100\".\n4. Consistent.\n- **441 vs 433 sessions**: the method paper's 433 comes from a slightly different set of\n  required traces (it also loads pupil in some analyses and silently skips sessions that raise).\n  Our 18 exclusions are individually enumerated and all justified.\n\n### Check 5 — Edge cases\n- *Off-by-one at trial edges*: bin *i* spans `[t_beg + 0.02 i, t_beg + 0.02 (i+1))`; the last\n  bin ends exactly at `stimOn + 1.5`. Verified by `np.allclose` against `bincount2D` (Check 3)\n  and against `np.histogram` with explicit edges (Check 2). The input's time value is the bin\n  *centre*, so its range is [−0.49, +1.49] rather than [−0.5, +1.5].\n- *Trials at the start/end of a session*: handled by `get_behavior_per_interval`'s coverage\n  test — video/wheel traces often start after the first trials, and those trials are dropped.\n- *Blocks*: `trial_number_in_block` is computed on the **full** trials table, restarts at 0 on\n  every `probabilityLeft` change, and reaches at most 98 (blocks are 20–100 trials) with the\n  first block reaching 89 (90 unbiased trials). This is the best\n  available 3-way split of that trace and is reported in the metadata.\n- *Unexpected `probabilityLeft`*: the conversion raises if any value is not 0.2/0.5/0.8 — never\n  triggered on the 459 sessions.\n- *Clusters with no spikes*: excluded, matching the reference `bin_spiking_data`.\n- *`min_rt` sign*: the reference query uses strict `<`/`>` so trials at exactly 0.08 s or 2.0 s\n  are kept; the port reproduces the query string verbatim and the masks are bit-identical.\n\n### Iterations\n1. **Iteration 1** — first full conversion gave 444 sessions with 38 \"all neural data is zero\"\n   warnings and a session containing a single neuron. *Fix*: added the data paper's\n   \"≥ 5 well-isolated neurons per session\" criterion (`MIN_NEURONS_PER_SESSION = 5`).\n   *Re-check*: 441 sessions, warnings down to 16, min neurons/session 7; all sanity checks and\n   reference comparisons re-run and pass.\n\n---\n\n## Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --plot-samples`\n→ `/app/train_decoder_full_out.txt`, `sample_trials.png`, `predictions.png`.\nRan on the GPU, 200 epochs, ~13 min.\n\n### Training Progress\n- Loss decreasing: **Yes**, monotonically — 2.645 (epoch 1) → 1.965 (10) → 1.557 (20) →\n  1.169 (40) → 0.806 (100) → 0.7477 (200). Three of the four outputs are ≥ 1.76× chance. `choice` is 1.23×\nchance, below the 1.5× flag, so it was investigated in depth.\n\n**Why `choice` cannot reach 1.5× chance on this task definition, and why that is not a bug.**\n`choice` is a *per-trial* label that the decoder must predict at **every one of the 100 time\nbins**, and the trial window starts 0.5 s *before* the stimulus appears. Choice information\nsimply does not exist in the brain during the first quarter of the window. Two independent\nanalyses of the time course confirm this (`/app/timecourse_analysis.py`, and a\ncompletely independent scikit-learn per-bin logistic regression on 25 sessions):\n\n| time from stimOn | decoder balanced acc (choice) | independent per-bin logistic (choice) |\n|---|---|---|\n| −0.49 … −0.01 s (pre-stimulus) | 0.570 | 0.52 |\n| +0.15 s | 0.662 | 0.72 |\n| **+0.23 … +0.31 s (peak)** | **0.697** | **0.78** |\n| +0.55 s | 0.657 | 0.65 |\n| +1.43 s | 0.594 | 0.55 |\n| **mean over all 100 bins** | **0.616** | 0.600 |\n\nThe decoder's overall 0.614 is exactly the average of a curve that runs from chance before the\nstimulus to ~0.70 just after the first wheel movement. The peak time (+0.23 … +0.31 s) matches\nthe IBL median reaction time, i.e. **choice becomes decodable precisely when the animal moves**\n— strong independent evidence that the temporal alignment is correct. The decoder's own value\n(0.616 averaged over bins) also *exceeds* the independent baseline (0.600), so the conversion is\nnot losing information relative to a straightforward reimplementation.\n\nCorresponding time courses for the other outputs (peak / pre-stimulus balanced accuracy):\nprior 0.694 / 0.644, wheel speed 0.596 / 0.456, whisker ME 0.565 / 0.457 — all peak at\n+0.23 s, again consistent with stimulus-locked alignment. (Per-bin balanced accuracy is lower\nthan the pooled value for the two time-varying outputs because within a single early time bin\nthe \"medium\"/\"high\" classes are rare; pooling over bins restores balance.)\n\nThings that were considered and rejected as ways to raise `choice`:\n- *align to `firstMovement_times` instead* (what the data paper uses for choice decoding, and\n  what the method paper uses for dynamic behaviours) — the decoder task here explicitly\n  specifies \"Temporally align based on stimulus onset\", so this is not allowed;\n- *shorten the window to post-stimulus only* — would discard the reference window (−0.5, +1.5) s\n  that both the reference code and the method paper prescribe for choice;\n- *use all 621,733 units instead of the 75,708 well-isolated ones* — measured directly\n  (see Check 2 below): it raises per-bin choice decoding by only ~0.02 for an 8.4× larger\n  dataset, and is contrary to the data paper's neuron inclusion criteria.\n\n### Check 2 — Comparison to the reference papers\n\n| Variable | Reference paper number | Metric / window used there | This dataset | Comparable? |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| choice | method paper Fig. 2/5: single-session linear & RRR per region ≈ 0.55–0.80 accuracy; \"to avoid **ceiling effects from using all regions**, we decode each region separately\" | one label per trial, single region, window (−0.5, +1.5) s | 0.614 averaged over all 100 bins; **0.697 at the informative bins**; 0.78 with a per-bin logistic on all neurons of a session | partly — theirs is per-trial and per-region, ours is per-timestep and all-region. Our peak sits at the top of their per-region range, consistent with their remark that using all regions approaches ceiling. |\n| prior / block | method paper: AUC 0.66 (linear baseline) → 0.72 (single-session BMM-HMM) → 0.79 (multi-session) | AUC, binary L/R block, window (−0.6, −0.1) s pre-onset | 0.651 balanced accuracy over **3** classes (chance 0.333), 0.694 at peak | different metric (AUC on 2 classes vs balanced accuracy on 3) — ours is far above its chance level and includes the harder 0.5 class |\n| wheel speed | method paper: R² 0.24 (linear) → 0.55 (RRR (M)) | R² of the continuous signal, aligned to first movement, 0–1 s | 0.598 balanced accuracy over 3 classes (chance 0.333) | not directly comparable (R² vs 3-class accuracy; different alignment event). The trials table is already one row per trial by design.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. The AI applies the full set of trial curation criteria from the Zhang et al. reference code's `load_trials_and_mask` with `prepare_data`'s parameters:\n- NaN exclusion for `stimOn_times`, `choice`, `feedback_times`, `probabilityLeft`, `firstMovement_times`, `feedbackType`\n- Reaction time (firstMovement_times - stimOn_times) outside [0.08, 2.0] s\n- No-response trials (choice == 0) dropped\n- Trial length (feedback_times - goCue_times) > 10 s dropped\n- Additionally, trials whose wheel or whisker trace does not cover the full window are dropped (via `bin_behaviour`'s coverage check)\n\nii.\n```python\nMIN_RT, MAX_RT, MAX_TRIAL_LEN = 0.08, 2.0, 10.0\nNAN_EXCLUDE = ['stimOn_times', 'choice', 'feedback_times', 'probabilityLeft',\n               'firstMovement_times', 'feedbackType']\n\ndef load_trials_and_mask(one, eid, sess_loader=None):\n    ...\n    query = f'(firstMovement_times - stimOn_times < {MIN_RT})'\n    query += f' | (firstMovement_times - stimOn_times > {MAX_RT})'\n    query += f' | (feedback_times - goCue_times > {MAX_TRIAL_LEN})'\n    for event in NAN_EXCLUDE:\n        query += f' | {event}.isnull()'\n    query += ' | (choice == 0)'\n    mask = ~trials.eval(query)\n    return trials, mask.to_numpy()\n```\n\nAnd behaviour coverage:\n```python\nkeep &= beh_good[name]  # trials where behaviour interpolation succeeded\n```\n\niii. The AI explicitly follows the Zhang reference code's `load_trials_and_mask` defaults plus the `max_trial_len=10.0` parameter from `prepare_data`. The behaviour coverage check mirrors `get_behavior_per_interval`'s trial rejection logic.\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. `spikes.times` and `spikes.clusters` from each probe's spike sorting, loaded via `SpikeSortingLoader`. The cluster table provides the QC label and anatomical region for filtering.\n\nii.\n```python\nloader = SpikeSortingLoader(pid=pid, one=one, eid=eid, pname=pname)\nspikes, clusters, channels = loader.load_spike_sorting()\nclusters_labeled = SpikeSortingLoader.merge_clusters(spikes, clusters, channels).to_df()\n```\n\niii. These are the standard spike sorting outputs from the IBL pipeline.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Spikes are counted into 20 ms bins over the trial window [-0.5, 1.5] s relative to stimulus onset, giving one count per unit per bin. The counts are stored as **spike counts** (float32), NOT divided by the bin width (i.e., not converted to firing rates). When a session has multiple probes, their units are concatenated into one population.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef bin_spikes(times, clusters, n_clusters, interval_begs, binsize=BINSIZE, n_bins=N_BINS):\n    ...\n    t_rel = times[flat_idx] - interval_begs[trial_of_spike]\n    bin_of_spike = np.floor(t_rel / binsize).astype(np.int64)\n    np.clip(bin_of_spike, 0, n_bins - 1, out=bin_of_spike)\n    lin = ((trial_of_spike * n_clusters) + clusters[flat_idx]) * n_bins + bin_of_spike\n    out += np.bincount(lin, minlength=n_trials * n_clusters * n_bins).reshape(\n        n_trials, n_clusters, n_bins).astype(np.float32)\n    return out\n```\n\nProbes concatenated:\n```python\nneural = np.concatenate(neural_trials, axis=1)  # (n_trials, n_neurons, T)\n```\n\niii. The AI documents that the binning arithmetic matches `bincount2D` as used by the reference `get_spike_data_per_interval`, and was verified to be bit-identical. The choice to keep spike counts (rather than converting to Hz) follows the Zhang reference code, which also stores counts.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Three filters applied:\n1. Only clusters with `label >= 1` (the \"well-isolated\" units from the IBL's RIGOR QC metrics)\n2. Clusters whose Beryl acronym is `root` or `void` are dropped (grey matter restriction)\n3. Clusters that emit no spikes in the session are dropped\nAdditionally, sessions with fewer than 5 well-isolated grey-matter neurons are dropped entirely.\n\nii.\n```python\nQC_LABEL = 1.0\nNON_GREY_MATTER = ('root', 'void')\nMIN_NEURONS_PER_SESSION = 5\n\nberyl = np.asarray(br.acronym2acronym(clusters_df['acronym'].to_numpy(), mapping='Beryl'), dtype=object)\nhas_spikes = np.zeros(len(clusters_df), dtype=bool)\nif clu.size:\n    has_spikes[np.unique(clu)] = True\nsel = (~np.isin(beryl, NON_GREY_MATTER)) & has_spikes\n\nif neural.shape[1] < MIN_NEURONS_PER_SESSION:\n    raise RuntimeError(f'only {neural.shape[1]} well-isolated grey-matter neurons')\n```\n\niii. The AI justifies `label >= 1` by citing the data paper's explicit neuron inclusion criteria (75,708 well-isolated neurons). The grey-matter restriction (dropping both `root` and `void`) is justified by the data paper's statement that analyses are restricted to grey-matter regions. The `has_spikes` filter matches the reference `bin_spiking_data` which uses `np.unique(regclu)`. The MIN_NEURONS=5 threshold is cited from the data paper.\n\n## 2-d.
